People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. I manage properties, and usually I can look at a board packet or a vendor email and make a decision pretty quickly, but now my head feels kind of cloudy. Even simple things take me longer than they should. I don’t know if that’s the right kind of thing to come in for, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really sad, I don’t think. Maybe a little discouraged because I’m not keeping up the way I usually do, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling down exactly. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? No, I don’t think so. I mean, I worry a bit because I’m not as on top of things as I usually am, but I wouldn’t say I feel like the future is hopeless. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble remembering things lately?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… I don’t know if it’s exactly remembering or more like my head gets cloudy. I’ll be looking at a board packet or a vendor email, something I normally handle pretty fast, and I just kind of stare at it and can’t decide what to do. Even simple things feel slower than they should. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t think so, not really. I mean, I get a little unsettled when I’m not getting things done the way I should, but I wouldn’t call it anxiety exactly. Maybe just normal worry? I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any loss of interest in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yes. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but I haven’t really wanted to go to my dance class, or stop in at the little galleries like I usually do. Even cooking Sunday food for my family… I’ve been putting it off, and those are things that usually make me feel like myself. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been having a lower mood than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… maybe a little lower, but I don’t want to overstate it. I wouldn’t say I’m falling apart or anything. Just not quite as bright or steady as I usually am, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I haven’t really been eating regular meals the way I should. Some days it’s just coffee and a few bites, and then later at night I’ll pick at a tortilla or something sweet, but I don’t even enjoy it much. I don’t know if that counts as a weight change exactly… I haven’t been keeping close track.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I get tired like anybody does, but I haven’t noticed it being something big or different enough to point to. Maybe I’m just moving a little slower some days, but… I don’t know if I’d call it more tired than usual.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating on things for a while now?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s actually the main thing that made me come in. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but my head feels kind of cloudy. Does that sound like what you mean by concentrating?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like a burden to your family or friends lately?\n\nPatient: A burden… I don’t know if I’d say it that strongly. I try not to put things on my family or friends. But I have been feeling bad about the residents, like I let them down. I spoke too big about what we could do and promised things before really checking the money first. Nobody’s told me I ruined anything, so maybe I’m making too much of it… but I keep turning it over in my head. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I do feel guilty about how I handled things with the residents. I spoke too big, promised more than I probably should have before I really checked the money side of it. Nobody’s said I ruined anything, and maybe I’m being too hard on myself, but I keep thinking I let them down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself lately?\n\nPatient: I… no, not hurting myself, not like that. I don’t want to do anything to myself, and I don’t have any plan. But… a few mornings I’ve had this scary thought, like maybe it would just be easier if I didn’t wake up. I feel bad even saying that. Is that the kind of thing you need to know?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these difficulties with concentration and decision-making?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been about two weeks… maybe a little more, but around two weeks. It seemed to get sharper after my husband retired and was suddenly home all day. I don’t want to blame him, it’s not his fault, but that’s about when my energy and confidence changed, and then this low, cloudy feeling made it harder to concentrate and decide things.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing persistent feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it worthless exactly… that sounds so strong. But I do keep going over how I handled things with the residents, like I talked too big and promised things before I really checked the money. Nobody has said I ruined anything, but I keep feeling like I let them down. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
